. We will step-by-step modify the given UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance un-
(X,t)
, and Lemma
20
follows directly after all the modification operations.

Step 1: Scaling and Grouping.

We scale
t
and each element in
X
by
\epsilon^{3}t
. To be specific, we scale
t
to
\bar{t}=t\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}t}
and scale each
x_{i}\in X
to
\bar{x}_{i}=x_{i}\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}t}
. Let
\overline{X}=\{\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\cdots,\bar{x}_{n}\}
. Notice that the optimal objective value of UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance un-
(\overline{X},\bar{t})
is at least
\frac{OPT}{\epsilon^{3}t}
.

Observe that there is a one-to-one correspondence between
X
and
\overline{X}
:
x\leftrightarrow\frac{x}{\epsilon^{3}t}
for any
x\in X
. We define the mapping
\Psi_{1}
from
\overline{X}
to
X
as follows: given any
y\in\overline{X}
, mapping
\Psi_{1}
returns
\epsilon^{3}ty
. Denote by
\Psi_{1}(y)
the element returned by
\Psi_{1}
given
y\in\overline{X}
.

Note that
\overline{X}\subset[\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}},\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}})
, we can divide
\overline{X}
into
\eta={\mathcal{O}}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
groups, denoted by
\overline{X}_{1},\overline{X}_{2},\cdots\overline{X}_{\eta}
, such that
\bar{x}\in\overline{X}_{j}
if and only if
x\in[\frac{2^{j-1}}{\epsilon^{2}},\frac{2^{j}}{\epsilon^{2}})\cap\overline{X}
.

The total processing time of Step 1 is
{\mathcal{O}}(|X|)
.

Step 2. Rounding and Further Grouping.

Consider each
\overline{X}_{j}
, note that
\overline{X}_{j}\subset[\frac{2^{j-1}}{\epsilon^{2}},\frac{2^{j}}{\epsilon^{2}}]=[\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2+\lambda_{j}}},\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2+\lambda_{j}}}]
, where
0\leq\lambda_{j}<1
. Recall Lemma
6
, given
d=1
and
\alpha_{j}=1+\lambda_{j}
, in
{\mathcal{O}}\left((|\overline{X}_{j}|+\frac{1}{\epsilon})\log|\overline{X}_{j}|\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)
time, we can obtain a set
\Delta_{j}\subset\mathbb{R}
with
\Delta_{j}\subset\Theta(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{1+\lambda_{j}}})
and
|\Delta_{j}|={\mathcal{O}}(\log|\overline{X}_{j}|\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
, moreover, we can round every
\bar{x}\in\overline{X}_{j}
to the form
\rho h
where
\rho\in\Delta_{j}
and
h
satisfying the following conditions:

\displaystyle h\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\cap[\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{\epsilon}];

(6a)

\displaystyle|\bar{x}-\rho h|\leq\epsilon\bar{x}.

(6b)

Denote by
\hat{X}_{j}
the set of all such rounded elements obtained from
\overline{X}_{j}
. Let
Z_{j}=set\{\hat{X}_{j}\}
and let
\hat{t}=(1+\epsilon)\bar{t}
. We have
Z_{j}\subset[\frac{2^{j-1}(1-\epsilon)}{\epsilon^{2}},\frac{2^{j}(1+\epsilon)}{\epsilon^{2}}]
. Recall that the optimal objective value of UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance un-
(\overline{X},\bar{t})
is at least
\frac{OPT}{\epsilon^{3}t}
. Condition (
6b
) guarantees that the optimal objective value of UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance un-
(set({\cup}^{\eta}_{j=1}Z_{j}),\hat{t})
is at least
\frac{(1-\epsilon)OPT}{\epsilon^{3}t}
.

Notice that for each
\hat{X}_{j}
, there is a one-to-one correspondence between
\overline{X}_{j}
and
\hat{X}_{j}
:
\bar{y}\leftrightarrow\hat{y}
for any
\bar{y}\in\overline{X}_{j}
, where
\hat{y}
is the factorized form of
\bar{y}
obtained after the above rounding procedure. For each
\hat{y}\in Z_{j}
, let
\mathcal{M}^{j}_{\hat{y}}
denote the set of all numbers in
\overline{X}_{j}
factorized to the form
\hat{y}
. We define the mapping
\Psi^{j}_{2}
from
Z_{j}
to
\overline{X}_{j}
as follows: given any
\hat{y}\in Z_{j}
, mapping
\Psi^{j}_{2}
returns any one element in
\mathcal{M}^{j}_{\hat{y}}
. Denote by
\Psi^{j}_{2}(\hat{y})
the element returned by
\Psi^{j}_{2}
given
\hat{y}\in Z_{j}
.

For each
Z_{j}
, let
\Delta_{j}=\{\rho^{j}_{1},\rho^{j}_{2},\cdots,\rho^{j}_{|\Delta_{j}|}\}
, where
|\Delta_{j}|={\mathcal{O}}(\log|\overline{X}_{j}|\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
. We further divide
Z_{j}
into
|\Delta_{j}|
groups, denoted by
Z^{j}_{1},Z^{j}_{2},\cdots,Z^{j}_{|\Delta_{j}|}
, such that
y\in Z^{j}_{k}
if and only
y
is of the form
\rho^{j}_{k}h
.

The total preprocessing time of Step 2 is
{\mathcal{O}}((|X|+\frac{1}{\epsilon})(\log|X|)^{2}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
.

Step 3. Reduce the UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM to BOUNDED SUBSET SUM.

Note that
\hat{t}=\frac{1+\epsilon}{\epsilon^{3}}
and
Z^{j}_{k}\in[\frac{2^{j-1}(1-\epsilon)}{\epsilon^{2}},\frac{2^{j}(1+\epsilon)}{\epsilon^{2}}]
for every
Z^{j}_{k}
, thus any optimal solution of
\text{un-}(set(\dot{\cup}^{\eta}_{j=1}Z_{j}),\hat{t})
contains at most
n^{j}_{k}=\lfloor\frac{\hat{t}}{(Z^{j}_{k})^{\min}}\rfloor
elements from
Z^{j}_{k}
. Note that elements in
Z^{j}_{k}
are different from each other. For every
Z^{j}_{k}
, we define a multiset
Y^{j}_{k}
such that
set\{Y^{j}_{k}\}=Z^{j}_{k}
and elements in
Y^{j}_{k}
have the same multiplicity of
l^{j}_{k}
, where

\displaystyle l^{j}_{k}=\begin{cases}&2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\hskip 25.60747pt\ \text{if }n^{j}_{k}\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\\
&2n^{j}_{k}\cdot 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\hskip 5.69054pt\ \text{if }n^{j}_{k}>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\end{cases}

Let
Y=\mathop{\dot{\cup}}\limits_{1\leq j\leq\eta;1\leq k\leq|\Delta_{j}|}Y^{j}_{k}
. Note that any optimal solution of BOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance
(Y,\hat{t})
is a optimal solution of UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance
(set({\cup}^{\eta}_{j=1}Z_{j}),\hat{t})
, vice versa. Thus the optimal objective value of
(Y,\hat{t})
is at least
\frac{(1-\epsilon)OPT}{\epsilon^{3}t}
.

Observe that the time to obtain
Y^{j}_{k}
from
Z^{j}_{k}
is
{\mathcal{O}}(|Z^{j}_{k}|\log|Z^{j}_{k}|)
. Thus the total processing time of Step 3 is
{\mathcal{O}}(|X|\log|X|)
.

Till now, we complete the preprocessing procedure and the overall time for preprocessing UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance
(X,t)
is
{\mathcal{O}}((|X|+\frac{1}{\epsilon})(\log|X|)^{2}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
. This finishes the first half (item (i)) of Lemma
20
. In the following, we prove the second half (item (ii)) of Lemma
20
.

Modified instance after preprocessing.

To summarize, we have reduced the UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance un-
(X,t)
to a BOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance
(Y,\hat{t})
satisfying the following conditions:

(A)

\hat{t}=(1+\epsilon)\epsilon^{-3}

(B)

The optimal objective value of
(Y,\hat{t})
is at least
\frac{(1-\epsilon)OPT}{\epsilon^{3}t}
.

(C)

Y
has been divided into
{\mathcal{O}}(\log|X|(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
groups:
Y^{j}_{k}
’s, i.e.,
Y=\mathop{\dot{\cup}}\limits_{j}(\mathop{\dot{\cup}}\limits_{k}Y^{j}_{k})
.

(D)

set(Y^{j}_{k})
is explicit given for every
Y^{j}_{k}
and
\sum_{j}\sum_{k}|set(Y^{j}_{k})|\leq|X|
.

(E)

Each subgroup
Y^{j}_{k}
satisfies the following conditions:

(a)

Y^{j}_{k}\subset[\frac{2^{j-1}(1-\epsilon)}{\epsilon^{2}},\frac{2^{j}(1+\epsilon)}{\epsilon^{2}}]
;

(b)

Y^{j}_{k}=\rho^{j}_{k}\overline{Y}^{j}_{k}
, where
\rho^{j}_{k}=\Omega(\epsilon^{-1})
and
\overline{Y}^{j}_{k}\subset\mathbb{N}_{+}\cap[\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{\epsilon}]
;

(c)

Let
n^{j}_{k}=\lfloor\frac{\hat{t}}{(Y^{j}_{k})^{\min}}\rfloor
and let

\displaystyle l^{j}_{k}=\begin{cases}&2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\hskip 25.60747pt\text{if }n^{j}_{k}\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\\
&2n^{j}_{k}\cdot 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\hskip 5.69054pt\text{if }n^{j}_{k}>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\end{cases}

Y^{j}_{k}
consists of
l^{j}_{k}
copies of
set(Y^{j}_{k})
, i.e., every element in
Y^{j}_{k}
has the same multiplicity, which is
l^{j}_{k}
.

Oracle for backtracking from
Y
to
X
.

We now present the oracle for backtracking from
Y
to
X
. For each
Y^{j}_{k}
, given
y\in Y^{j}_{k}
, the oracle works as follows:

•

Recall that
Y^{j}_{k}\subset Z_{j}
and
\Psi^{j}_{2}
is a mapping from
Z_{j}
to
\overline{X}_{j}
. The oracle uses
\Psi^{j}_{2}
to first obtain
\Psi^{j}_{2}(y)
. Observe that
|\Psi^{j}_{2}(y)-y|\leq\epsilon\Psi^{j}_{2}(y)
and
\Psi^{j}_{2}(y)\in\overline{X}
.

•

Recall that
\Psi_{1}
is a mapping from
\overline{X}
to
X
. Then the oracle uses
\Psi_{1}
to obtain and return
\Psi_{1}(\Psi^{j}_{2}(y))
. Note that
\Psi_{1}(\Psi^{j}_{2}(y))\in X
and
\Psi_{1}(\Psi^{j}_{2}(y))=\epsilon^{3}t\Psi^{j}_{2}(y)
.

Let
x^{\prime}=\Psi_{1}(\Psi^{j}_{2}(y))
. The above backtracking procedure only takes
{\mathcal{O}}(1)
time. To summarize, we have
|x^{\prime}-\epsilon^{3}ty|=\epsilon^{3}t\cdot|\Psi^{j}_{2}(y)-y|\leq\epsilon^{3}t\cdot\epsilon\Psi^{j}_{2}(y)=\epsilon x^{\prime}
.

Till now, we have completed the proof of Lemma
20
.

8.2
Algorithm for UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM.

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
4
. Given any UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance
\text{un-}(X,t)
, where
X=\{x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n}\}
is a set of distinct positive integers and
t>0
is a fixed constant. Let
OPT
be the optimal objective value of
\text{un-}(X,t)
. Our goal is to find a weak
(1-\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))
-approximation solution for
\text{un-}(X,t)
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
0<x\leq(1+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))t
for every
x\in X
. Furthermore, according to Lemma
18
, we may assume that
\epsilon t<x<t
for every
x\in X
. Then by Lemma
20
, in
{\mathcal{O}}((n+\frac{1}{\epsilon})(\log n)^{2}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
time, we can obtain a bounded SUBSET SUM instance
(Y,\hat{t})
satisfying conditions (A)(B)(C)(D)(E) and meanwhile build an oracle for backtracking from
Y
to
X
(see Lemma
20
in Section
8.1
). Condition (B) and the backtracking oracle guarantee that towards proving Theorem
4
, it is sufficient to consider
(Y,\hat{t})
. Precisely, Theorem
4
follows directly from the following Lemma
21
.

Lemma 21
.

Give any UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance
\text{un-}(X,t)
where
\epsilon t<x<t
for every
x\in X
. Let
(Y,\hat{t})
be the modified instance returned by Lemma
20
, where
Y
is the multiset-union of all
Y^{j}_{k}
’s.
Then in
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(|X|+\epsilon^{-1})
processing time, we can determine
U^{j}_{k}\subset Y^{j}_{k}
for every
U^{j}_{k}
such that

(i).

For each
U^{j}_{k}
,
card_{U^{j}_{k}}[y]
is explicit given for every
y\in set(Y^{j}_{k})
.

(ii).

The multiset-union of all
U^{j}_{k}
’s is a weak
(1-\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))
-approximation solution for
(Y,\hat{t})
.

Let
Y_{\leq\hat{t}}=\{y\in Y:y\leq\hat{t}\}
and let
Y^{(j;k)}_{\leq\hat{t}}=\{y\in Y^{j}_{k}:y\leq\hat{t}\}
for every
Y^{j}_{k}
, it holds that
Y_{\leq\hat{t}}=\mathop{\dot{\cup}}\limits_{j}(\mathop{\dot{\cup}}\limits_{k}Y^{(j;k)}_{\leq\hat{t}})
. Recall Lemma
20
,
Y^{j}_{k}
is
l^{j}_{k}
copies of
set(Y^{j}_{k})
, then
card_{Y^{(j;k)}_{\leq\bar{t}}}[y]=l^{j}_{k}
for every
y\in Y^{(j;k)}_{\leq\bar{t}}
. Note that
set(Y^{(j;k)}_{\leq\hat{t}})=set(Y^{j}_{k})\cap[0,\hat{t}]
.
Thus in
{\mathcal{O}}(\mathop{\sum}\limits_{j}\mathop{\sum}\limits_{k}|set(Y^{j}_{k})|)={\mathcal{O}}(|X|)
time, we can obtain
Y^{(j;k)}_{\leq\hat{t}}
for every
Y^{j}_{k}
, furthermore, we can obtain
\Sigma(Y_{\leq\hat{t}})=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{j}\mathop{\sum}\limits_{k}\left(l^{j}_{k}\Sigma(set(Y^{(j;k)}_{\leq\hat{t}}))\right)
.

Let
OPT^{*}
be the optimal objective value of
(Y,\hat{t})
. Recall Lemma
1
, if
\Sigma(Y_{\leq\hat{t}})<{\hat{t}}/{2}
, then
OPT^{*}<{\hat{t}}/{2}
and
Y_{\leq\hat{t}}
is an optimal solution of
(Y,\hat{t})
, else if
\Sigma(Y_{\leq\hat{t}})\geq{\hat{t}}/{2}
, we can assert that
OPT^{*}\geq{\hat{t}}/{2}
. Thus we only need to consider the case that
OPT^{*}\geq{\hat{t}}/{2}
.

In the following, we assume that
OPT^{*}\geq{\hat{t}}/{2}
. Then a subset
Y^{\prime}\subset Y
satisfying
|\Sigma(Y^{\prime})-OPT^{*}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\hat{t}
is a weak
(1-\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))
-approximation solution of
(Y,\hat{t})
. Recall Lemma
20
,
set(Y^{j}_{k})
is explicit given for every
Y^{j}_{k}
and
\sum_{j}\sum_{k}|set(Y^{j}_{k})|\leq|X|
. Thus the following Lemma
22
implies Lemma
21
directly.

Lemma 22
.

Give any UNBOUNDED SUBSET SUM instance
\text{un-}(X,t)
where
\epsilon t<x<t
for every
x\in X
. Let
(Y,\hat{t})
be the modified instance returned by Lemma
20
, where
Y
is the multiset-union of all
Y^{j}_{k}
’s.
Then in
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
processing time, we can

(i).

Compute an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\hat{t})
-approximate set
C
with cardinality of
{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
for
S(Y)
.

(ii).

Meanwhile build an
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
-time oracle for backtracking from
C
to
Y
. Here
Y
is the multiset-union of all
Y^{j}_{k}
’s and the oracle actually works as follows: given any
c\in C
, in
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
time, the oracle will return
U^{j}_{k}\subset Y^{k}_{j}
for every
Y^{k}_{j}
, where
card_{U^{j}_{k}}[y]
is explicit given for every
y\in set(Y^{j}_{k})
. Let
Y^{\prime}
be the multiset-union of all
U^{j}_{k}
’s, we have
Y^{\prime}\subset Y
and
|\Sigma(Y^{\prime})-c|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\hat{t}
.

The rest of this section is dedicated to proving Lemma
22
. Recall Corollary
2
, towards proving Lemma
22
, we only need to derive an algorithm that can solve the following
problem
-
\mathscr{F}
in
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
-time.

problem
-
\mathscr{F}
: for every
Y^{j}_{k}
, compute a
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\hat{t})
-approximate set with cardinality of
{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
for
S(Y^{j}_{k})
, and build an
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
-time oracle for backtracking from this approximate set to
Y^{j}_{k}
.

Note that all
Y^{j}_{k}
’s satisfy Condition (B) (see Lemma
20
). To solve
problem
-
\mathscr{F}
, we only need to prove the following Lemma
23
, where
M
represents an arbitrary
Y^{j}_{k}
.

Lemma 23
.

Given
\omega\in\Theta(\epsilon^{-3})
and a multiset
M\subset\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
satisfying the following conditions:

1.

M=\beta\overline{M}
, where
\beta\in\Omega(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
and
\overline{M}\subset\mathbb{N}_{+}\cap[\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{\epsilon}]
.

2.

set(M)
is explicitly given.

3.

Let
m=\lfloor\frac{\omega}{M^{\min}}\rfloor
and let

\displaystyle l_{M}=\begin{cases}&2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\hskip 25.60747pt\text{if }m\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\\
&2m\cdot 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\hskip 5.69054pt\text{if }m>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\end{cases}

M
consists of
l_{M}
copies of
set(M)
, i.e., every element in
M
has the same multiplicity, which is
l_{M}
.

In
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
processing time, we can

(i).

Compute an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\omega)
-approximate set
C_{M}
with cardinality of
{\mathcal{O}}({\epsilon^{-1}})
for
S(M)
.

(ii).

Meanwhile build an
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
-time oracle for backtracking from
C_{M}
to
M
. That is, given any
c\in C_{M}
, in
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
time, the oracle will return
M^{\prime}\subset M
such that
|\Sigma(M^{\prime})-c|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\omega
. Moreover,
card_{M^{\prime}}[y]
for every
y\in set(M)
are also returned by the oracle.

Let
\upsilon=\frac{\omega}{\beta}
and let
G:=set(\overline{M})=\frac{set(M)}{\beta}
, it is easy to see that
\upsilon={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}})
and
\lfloor\frac{\upsilon}{G^{\min}}\rfloor=\lfloor\frac{\omega}{M^{\min}}\rfloor=m={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
.
Observe that given any
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set of
S(\overline{M})
, say
C_{\overline{M}}
, then
\beta C_{\overline{M}}
is an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\omega)
-approximate set of
S(M)
, furthermore, a
T
-time oracle for backtracking from
C_{\overline{M}}
to
\overline{M}
will directly yield an
{\mathcal{O}}(T)
-time oracle for backtracking from
\beta C_{\overline{M}}
to
M
. It thus suffices to consider
\overline{M}
and
\upsilon
. Precisely, we only need to derive an
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
-time algorithm for computing an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set with cardinality of
{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
for
S(\overline{M})
and meanwhile build an
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
-time oracle for backtracking from this approximate set to
\overline{M}
. Two cases,
m\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
and
m>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
, will be considered in Section
8.2.1
and Section
8.2.2
separately.

Let
G_{\log}=\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}
. Then
2^{k}G_{\log}=\{2^{k}x:x\in G_{\log}\}
for
k=1,2,\cdots,pow(m)
. Before proceeding, we first present the following useful observations.

Observation 9
.

When
m\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
, we have
G_{\log}\subset S(\overline{M})
, moreover, we have
G_{\log}\cap[0,\upsilon]=S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]
.

Proof.

When
m\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
, we have
l_{M}=2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
. Note that
G=set(\overline{M})
and elements in
\overline{M}
have the same multiplicity of
l_{M}
. It follows that
G_{\log}=set\{\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime}):\overline{M}^{\prime}\subset\overline{M}\text{ \ and\ }|\overline{M}^{\prime}|\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\}
. Thus
G_{\log}\subset S(\overline{M})
, furthermore, we have
G_{\log}\cap[0,\upsilon]\subset S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]
.

It remains to prove that
S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]\subset G_{\log}\cap[0,\upsilon]
. Consider any
s\in S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]
, there exists
\overline{M}^{\prime\prime}\subset\overline{M}
such that
s=\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime\prime})
. Notice that
|\overline{M}^{\prime\prime}|\cdot G^{\min}\leq\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime\prime})\leq\upsilon
, we have
|\overline{M}^{\prime\prime}|\leq\lfloor\frac{\upsilon}{G^{\min}}\rfloor=m\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
, which implies that
s=\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime\prime})\in G_{\log}
. Hence
S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]\subset G_{\log}\cap[0,\upsilon]
. ∎∎

Observation 10
.

When
m>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
, we have
(G_{\log}\oplus 2G_{\log}\oplus\cdots\oplus 2^{pow(m)}G_{\log})\subset S(\overline{M})
. Moreover, we have
S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]=(G_{\log}\oplus 2G_{\log}\oplus\cdots\oplus 2^{pow(m)}G_{\log})\cap[0,\upsilon]
.

Proof.

When
m>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
, we have
l_{M}=2m\cdot 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
. Note that
G=set(\overline{M})
and elements in
\overline{M}
have the same multiplicity of
l_{M}
. It follows that

\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2m\cdot 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}=set\{\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime}):\overline{M}^{\prime}\subset\overline{M}\text{ \ and\ }|\overline{M}^{\prime}|\leq 2m\cdot 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}\}\subset S(\overline{M}).

Observe that
G_{\log}\oplus 2G_{\log}\oplus\cdots\oplus 2^{pow(m)}G_{\log}\subset\underbrace{G_{\log}\oplus G_{\log}\oplus\cdots\oplus G_{\log}}_{2m}=\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2m\cdot 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}\subset S(\overline{M})
, furthermore, we have
(G_{\log}\oplus 2G_{\log}\oplus\cdots\oplus 2^{pow(m)}G_{\log})\cap[0,\upsilon]\subset S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]
.

It remains to prove that
S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]\subset(G_{\log}\oplus 2G_{\log}\oplus\cdots\oplus 2^{pow(m)}G_{\log})\cap[0,\upsilon]
. Let
G=\{g_{1},g_{2},\cdots,g_{|G|}\}
. For any
s\in S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]
, note that
G=set(\overline{M})
, then
s\in S(G)\cap[0,\upsilon]
. Recall Lemma
19
, there exists
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}=\{y_{1},y_{2},\cdots,y_{|G|}\}\in\mathbb{N}^{|G|}
such that
s=\sum^{|G|}_{i=1}g_{i}y_{i}
, and
\textstyle\bf y
contains at most
\log_{2}(G^{\min})+1
non-zero components. Notice that
G^{\min}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}
, we have
\log_{2}(G^{\min})+1\leq\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1
. Observe that
\sum^{|G|}_{i=1}y_{i}\leq\lfloor\frac{\sum^{|G|}_{i=1}g_{i}y_{i}}{G^{\min}}\rfloor\leq\lfloor\frac{\upsilon}{G^{\min}}\rfloor=m
, we have
y_{i}\leq m
for every
i
. Then each
y_{i}
can be written as
y_{i}=\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}\gamma_{k}(y_{i})\cdot 2^{k}
, where
\gamma_{k}(y_{i})\in\{0,1\}
for
k=0,1,2,\cdots,pow(m)
. Note that if
y_{i}=0
, then
\gamma_{k}(y_{i})=0
for every
k
. Thus the fact that
\textstyle\bf y
contains at most
(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)
non-zero components implies that
(\gamma_{k}(y_{1}),\gamma_{k}(y_{2}),\cdots,\gamma_{k}(y_{|G|}))
contains at most
(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)
non-zero components, futher implies that
\Sigma^{|G|}_{i=1}\gamma_{k}(y_{i})g_{i}\in G_{\log}
.
Then we have
s=\sum^{|G|}_{i=1}g_{i}y_{i}=\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}(\Sigma^{|G|}_{i=1}\gamma_{k}(y_{i})g_{i})\in(G_{\log}\oplus 2G_{\log}\oplus\cdots\oplus 2^{pow(m)}G_{\log})\cap[0,\upsilon]
. ∎∎

8.2.1
Handling the case that
m\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
.

In this section, we consider the case that
m\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
. The following Claim
8
guarantees that towards proving Lemma
23
for the case
m\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
, it is sufficient to consider
G_{\log}
.

Claim 8
.

Assume that
m\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
. Let
C
be an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set for
G_{\log}=\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}
. Let
Ora
be a
T
-time oracle for backtracking from
C
to
\underbrace{G\dot{\cup}G\dot{\cup}\cdots\dot{\cup}G}_{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}
, that is, given any
c\in C
, within
T
time,
Ora
will return
x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}\in G\cup\{0\}

such that
|c-{\sum}^{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}_{i=1}x_{i}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
.

Then
C
is an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set for
S(\overline{M})
. Moreover,
Ora
directly yileds an
{\mathcal{O}}(T+\frac{1}{\epsilon})
-time oracle for backtracking from
C
to
\overline{M}
. That is, given any
c\in C
, by calling
Ora
, in
{\mathcal{O}}(T+\frac{1}{\epsilon})
time, we can obtain
\overline{M}^{\prime}\subset\overline{M}
such that
|c-\Sigma(\overline{M})^{\prime}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
, in particular,
card_{\overline{M}^{\prime}}[x]
is obtained for every
x\in set(\overline{M})
.

Proof.

When
m\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
, elements in
\overline{M}
have the same multiplicity of
l_{M}=2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
. Moreover, recall Observation
9
, we have
G_{\log}\subset S(\overline{M})
and
G_{\log}\cap[0,\upsilon]=S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]
.

Since
C
is an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set of
G_{\log}
, we observe the followings:

•

C\subset[0,(1+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))\upsilon]
.

•

For any
c\in C
, there exist
x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}\in G\cup\{0\}
such that
|c-\sum^{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}_{i=1}x_{i}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
. Note that
G=set(\overline{M})
and elements in
\overline{M}
have the same multiplicity of
2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
, we have
\{x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}\}\subset\overline{M}
.

•

For any
s\in S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]
, since
G_{\log}\cap[0,\upsilon]=S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]
, we have
s\in G_{\log}\cap[0,\upsilon]
. Then there exists
s^{\prime\prime}\in C
such that
|s-s^{\prime\prime}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
.

Thus
C
is an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set of
S(\overline{M})
.

Given
Ora
defined in Claim
8
. For any
c\in C
, within
T
time,
Ora
will return
x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}\in G\cup\{0\}
such that
|c-\sum^{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}_{i=1}x_{i}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
. Let
\overline{M}^{\prime}=\{x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}\}
. It is easy to see that
\overline{M}^{\prime}\subset\overline{M}
and the total time to determine
card_{\overline{M}^{\prime}}[x]
for every
x\in set(\overline{M})
is
{\mathcal{O}}(2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}+|G|)={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
. Thus
Ora
yields an
{\mathcal{O}}(T+\frac{1}{\epsilon})
-time oracle for backtracking from
C
to
\overline{M}
.∎∎

In the following, we will design an iterative approach to compute an approximate set for
G_{\log}
and meanwhile build an oracle for backtracking.

Approximating
\mathbf{G_{\log}}
.

Given any multiset
A
, recall that
A\dot{\cup}A
is the multiset that duplicates each element in
A
. We build a linked list structure as follows:

1.

At iteration-1, we use Observation
4
to compute an
(\epsilon,\min\{2G^{\max},\upsilon\})
-approximate set with cardinality of
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
for
G\oplus G
and meanwhile derive an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
-time oracle for backtracking from this approximate set to
G\dot{\cup}G
. Denote by
U^{1}
this approximate set.
We create a head node, and let the head node contain
U^{1}
and the oracle for backtracking from
U^{1}
to
G\dot{\cup}G
. Let
f^{1}(\epsilon):=\epsilon
.

Note that
G\subset[\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{\epsilon}]
and elements in
G
are different from each other, thus
|G|\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}
. According to Observation
4
, the total processing time at iteration-1 is
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
.

2.

Before proceeding to iteration-
h
, where
h\geq 2
, we assume that the following things have been down:

•

Let
U^{0}
denote
G
. We have obtained
U^{0},U^{1},U^{2},\cdots,U^{h-1}
, where
U^{k}
is an
\left(\epsilon,\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{k-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set with cardinality of
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
for
U^{k-1}\oplus U^{k-1}
and
k=1,2,\cdots,h-1
.

Functions in
\{f^{\iota}(\epsilon):\iota=0,1,\cdots,h-1\}
are defined by the following recurrence relation:

f^{0}(\epsilon)=0
and
f^{\iota}(\epsilon)=\epsilon+2(1+\epsilon)f^{\iota-1}(\epsilon)\text{ \ for \ }\iota=1,2,\cdots,k.

•

For each
U^{k}
, where
k=1,\cdots,h-1
, we have built an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
-time oracle for backtracking from
U^{k}
to
U^{k-1}\dot{\cup}U^{k-1}
. That is, given any
y\in U^{k}
, in
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})

time, the oracle will return
y_{1}\in U^{k-1}\cup\{0\}
and
y_{2}\in U^{k-1}\cup\{0\}
such that

|y-(y_{1}+y_{2})|\leq\epsilon\left(\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{k-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right).

Moreover, a node is created for containing
U^{k}
and the oracle for backtracking from
U^{k}
to
U^{k-1}\dot{\cup}U^{k-1}
.

Now we start iteration-
h
. We use Observation
4
to compute an
\left(\epsilon,\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set with cardinality of
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
for
U^{h-1}\oplus U^{h-1}
and meanwhile build an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
-time oracle for backtracking from this approximate set to
U^{h-1}\dot{\cup}U^{h-1}
. Denote by
U^{h}
this approximate set. Then we create a node behind the
(h-1)
-th node. Let this new node contain
U^{h}
and the oracle for backtracking from
U^{h}
to
U^{h-1}\dot{\cup}U^{h-1}
. Let
f^{h}(\epsilon):=\epsilon+2(1+\epsilon)f^{h-1}(\epsilon)
.

Notice that
|U^{h-1}|={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
. According to Observation
4
, the total processing time at iteration-
h
is
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
.

3.

Using the same approach in iteration-
h
recursively, and iteratively create linked nodes.

Let
h_{p}:=1+pow(1+\log_{2}\frac{1}{\epsilon})
. After
h_{p}
such rounds we stop and have built a linked list whose tail node contains (i).
U^{h_{p}}
, which is an
\left(\epsilon,\min\{2^{h_{p}}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h_{p}-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h_{p}}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set for
U^{h_{p}-1}\oplus U^{h_{p}-1}
;
(ii). an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
-time oracle for backtracking from
U^{h_{p}}
to
U^{h_{p}-1}\dot{\cup}U^{h_{p}-1}
. That is, given any
y\in U^{h_{p}}
, in
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})

time, the oracle will return
y_{1}\in U^{h_{p}-1}\cup\{0\}
and
y_{2}\in U^{h_{p}-1}\cup\{0\}
such that

|y-(y_{1}+y_{2})|\leq\epsilon\left(\min\{2^{h_{p}}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h_{p}-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h_{p}}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right).

To summarize, the overall processing time is
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{h_{p}}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
. Note that we have obtained
U^{0},U^{1},U^{2},\cdots,U^{h_{p}}
and defined
f^{0}(\epsilon),f^{1}(\epsilon),\cdots,f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)
. For every integer
1\leq h\leq h_{p}
,
U^{h}
is an
\left(\epsilon,\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\cdot\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set for
U^{h-1}\dot{\cup}U^{h-1}
, moreover,
|U^{h}|={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
. Functions in
\left\{f^{h}(\epsilon):h=0,1,2,\cdots,h_{p}\right\}
are defined by the following recurrence relation:

f^{0}(\epsilon)=0
and
f^{h}(\epsilon)=\epsilon+2(1+\epsilon)f^{h-1}(\epsilon)
for
h=1,2,\cdots,h_{p}
. A simple calculation shows that
f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)={\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
.

We claim that
U^{h_{p}}
is an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set with cardinality of
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
for
G_{\log}
. In particular, we claim the following.

Claim 9
.

For every
h=1,2,\cdots,h_{p}
,
U^{h}
is an
(f^{h}(\epsilon),\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\})
-approximate set for
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}
. In particular,
U^{h}
is an
(f^{h}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set for
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}
.

Proof.

We prove Claim
9
by induction.

Note that
\upsilon\geq\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}
and
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}\cap[0,\upsilon]=\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}\cap[0,\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}]
hold for every
h=1,2\cdots,h_{p}
. It is straightforward that an
(f^{h}(\epsilon),\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\})
-approximate set for
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}
is automatically an
(f^{h}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set for
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}
. So we only need to prove the first half of Claim
9
.

For
h=1
. Recall that
U^{1}
is an
\left(\epsilon,\min\{2^{1}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{0}(\epsilon)\cdot\min\{2^{1}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set for
U^{0}\dot{\cup}U^{0}
, where
f^{0}(\epsilon)=0
and
U^{0}=G
. Apparently,
U^{1}
is an
\left(\epsilon,\min\{2G^{\max},\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set for
G\dot{\cup}G
.

Then we consider any
h=2,3,\cdots,h_{p}
. Assume that
U^{h-1}
is
(f^{h-1}(\epsilon),\min\{2^{h-1}G^{\max},\upsilon\})
-approximate set of
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h-1}}
. In the following, we prove that
U^{h}
is an
(f^{h}(\epsilon),\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\})
-approximate set for
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}
. Recall that
U^{h}
is an
\left(\epsilon,\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set for
U^{h-1}\oplus U^{h-1}
and
f^{h}(\epsilon)=\epsilon+2(1+\epsilon)f^{h-1}(\epsilon)
. We have

\displaystyle(U^{h})^{\max}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon)\left(\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)

\displaystyle=(1+\epsilon)\left(\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+2f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h-1}G^{\max},\upsilon\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon)\left(\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+2f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}\right)

\displaystyle=(1+f^{h}(\epsilon))\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}.

Thus
U^{h}\subset[0,(1+f^{h}(\epsilon))\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}]
.

Given any
a\in\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}\cap[0,\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}]=\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}\cap[0,\upsilon]
, there exist
a_{1},a_{2}\in\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h-1}}\cap[0,\upsilon]=\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h-1}}\cap[0,\min\{2^{h-1}G^{\max},\upsilon\}]
such that
a=a_{1}+a_{2}
.
Recall that
U^{h-1}
is
(f^{h-1}(\epsilon),\min\{2^{h-1}G^{\max},\upsilon\})
-approximate set for
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h-1}}
, thus there exists
a^{\prime}_{i}\in U^{h-1}
such that
|a_{i}-a^{\prime}_{i}|\leq f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h-1}G^{\max},\upsilon\}
for
i=1,2
. Then we have
a^{\prime}_{1}+a^{\prime}_{2}\leq a+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\leq\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}.

Thus
a^{\prime}_{1}+a^{\prime}_{2}\in(U^{h-1}\oplus U^{h-1})\cap[0,\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}]
. Let
a^{\prime}=a^{\prime}_{1}+a^{\prime}_{2}
. Recall that
U^{h}
is an
(\epsilon,\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\})
-approximate set for
U^{h-1}\oplus U^{h-1}
, then there exists
a^{\prime\prime}\in U^{h}
such that

|a^{\prime\prime}-a^{\prime}|\leq\epsilon\left(\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right).
To summarize, given
a\in\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}\cap[0,\upsilon]
, there exist
a^{\prime\prime}\in U^{h}
such that

\displaystyle|a^{\prime\prime}-a|

\displaystyle\leq|a^{\prime\prime}-a^{\prime}|+|a^{\prime}-a|\leq|a^{\prime\prime}-a^{\prime}|+|a^{\prime}_{1}-a_{1}|+|a^{\prime}_{2}-a_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\left(\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)+2f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h-1}G^{\max},\upsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\epsilon+2(\epsilon+1)f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\right)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}=f^{h}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}.

Given any
c\in U^{h}
, note that
U^{h}
is an
\left(\epsilon,\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set for
U^{h-1}\oplus U^{h-1}
, then there exist
c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2}\in U^{h-1}\cup\{0\}
such that

|c-(c^{\prime}_{1}+c^{\prime}_{2})|\leq\epsilon\left(\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)
. Consider each
c^{\prime}_{i}
. If
c^{\prime}_{i}\in U^{h-1}
, recall that
U^{h-1}
is an
(f^{h-1}(\epsilon),\min\{2^{h-1}G^{\max},\upsilon\})
-approximate set for
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h-1}}
, then there exist
\bar{c}^{(i;1)},\bar{c}^{(i;2)},\cdots,\bar{c}^{(i;2^{h-1})}\in G\cup\{0\}
such that
|c^{\prime}_{i}-\sum^{2^{h-1}}_{j=1}\bar{c}^{(i;j)}|\leq f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h-1}G^{\max},\upsilon\}
. Else if
c^{\prime}_{i}=0
, let
\bar{c}^{(i;1)}=\bar{c}^{(i;2)}=\cdots=\bar{c}^{(i;2^{h-1})}=0
, apparently,
|c^{\prime}_{i}-\sum^{2^{h-1}}_{j=1}\bar{c}^{(i;j)}|\leq f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h-1}G^{\max},\upsilon\}
.
To summarize, given
c\in U^{h}
, there exist
\bar{c}^{(1;1)},\bar{c}^{(1;2)},\cdots,\bar{c}^{(1;2^{h-1})};\bar{c}^{(2;1)},\bar{c}^{(2;2)},\cdots,\bar{c}^{(2;2^{h-1})}\in G\cup\{0\}
such that

\displaystyle|c-\sum^{2}_{i=1}\sum^{2^{h-1}}_{j=1}\bar{c}^{(i;j)}|

\displaystyle\leq|c-(c^{\prime}_{1}+c^{\prime}_{2})|+|c^{\prime}_{1}-\Sigma^{2^{h-1}}_{j=1}\bar{c}^{(1;j)}|+|c^{\prime}_{2}-\Sigma^{2^{h-1}}_{j=1}\bar{c}^{(2;j)}|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\left(\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)+2f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h-1}G^{\max},\upsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq f^{h}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}.

Till now, we have proved that
U^{h}
is an
(f^{h}(\epsilon),\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\})
-approximate set of
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}
, which implies that
U^{h}
is an
(f^{h}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set for
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{h}}
.
Then Claim
9
follows by induction.∎∎

It remains to give the backtracking oracle. With the help of the linked list structure, an
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-1})
-time oracle
Ora
for backtracking from
U^{h_{p}}
to
\underbrace{G\dot{\cup}G\dot{\cup}\cdots\dot{\cup}G}_{2^{h_{p}}}
is derived and works as follows:

•

For any
c\in U^{h_{p}}
, we backtrace from the tail node to the head node. Note that
U^{h_{p}}
is an
\left(\epsilon,\min\{2^{h_{p}}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h_{p}-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h_{p}}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set for
U^{h_{p}-1}\oplus U^{h_{p}-1}
, and the tail node contains an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
-time oracle for backtracking from
U^{h_{p}}
to
U^{h_{p}-1}\dot{\cup}U^{h_{p}-1}
. Thus in
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
time, we can determine
c^{(h_{p}-1;1)},c^{(h_{p}-1;2)}\in U^{h_{p}-1}\cup\{0\}
such that

|c-(c^{(h_{p}-1;1)}+c^{(h_{p}-1;2)})|\leq\epsilon\left(\min\{2^{h_{p}}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h_{p}-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h_{p}}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right).

•

Back track recursively.

Given
1\leq h\leq h_{p}-1
, assume that we have determined
c^{(h;j_{h})}\in U^{h}{\cup}\{0\}
for every
j_{h}=1,2,3,\cdots,2^{h_{p}-h}
such that

|c-\sum^{2^{h_{p}-h}}_{j_{h}=1}c^{(h;j_{h})}|\leq\sum^{h_{p}}_{k=h+1}2^{h_{p}-k}\epsilon\left(\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{k-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right).

For each
c^{(h;j_{h})}\in U^{h}{\cup}\{0\}
, note that
U^{h}
is an
\left(\epsilon,\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set for
U^{h-1}\oplus U^{h-1}
, and the node containing
U^{h}
also contains an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
-time oracle for backtracking from
U^{h}
to
U^{h-1}\dot{\cup}U^{h-1}
. Thus in
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
time, we can determine
c^{(h-1;2j_{h}-1)},c^{(h-1;2j_{h})}\in U^{h-1}\cup\{0\}
such that

|c^{(h;j_{h})}-(c^{(h-1;2j_{h}-1)}+c^{(h-1;2j_{h})})|\leq\epsilon\left(\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right).

Then in total
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{2^{h_{p}-h}}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
time, we can determine
c^{(h-1;1)},c^{(h-1;2)},c^{(h-1;3)},\cdots,c^{(h-1;2^{h_{p}-h+1})}\in U^{h-1}\cup\{0\}
such that

\displaystyle|\sum^{2^{h_{p}-h}}_{j_{h}=1}c^{(h;j_{h})}-\sum^{2^{h_{p}-h+1}}_{j_{h-1}=1}c^{(h-1;j_{h-1})}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{2^{h_{p}-h}}_{j_{h}=1}|c^{(h;j_{h})}-(c^{(h-1;2j_{h}-1)}+c^{(h-1;2j_{h})})|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{h_{p}-h}\epsilon\left(\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{h-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right).

It follows that

|c-\sum^{2^{h_{p}-h+1}}_{j_{h-1}=1}c^{(h-1;j_{h-1})}|\leq\sum^{h_{p}}_{k=h}2^{h_{p}-k}\epsilon\cdot\left(\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{k-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right).

•

After
h_{p}
such rounds, we stop and have determined
c^{(1;1)},c^{(1;2)},c^{(1;3)},\cdots,c^{(1;2^{h_{p}})}\in G\cup\{0\}
such that

|c-\sum^{2^{h_{p}}}_{j_{1}=1}c^{(1;j_{1})}|\leq\sum^{h_{p}}_{k=1}2^{h_{p}-k}\epsilon\cdot\left(\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{k-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)

Observe that
\epsilon\left(\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{k-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)+2f^{k-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{k-1}G^{\max},\upsilon\}\leq(\epsilon+2(\epsilon+1)f^{k-1}(\epsilon))\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},\upsilon\}=f^{k}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},\upsilon\}
holds for every
k=1,2,\cdots,h_{p}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle|c-\sum^{2^{h_{p}}}_{j_{1}=1}c^{(1;j_{1})}|\leq\sum^{h_{p}}_{k=1}2^{h_{p}-k}\epsilon\cdot\left(\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{k-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum^{h_{p}}_{k=2}2^{h_{p}-k}\epsilon\cdot\left(\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{k-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)+2^{h_{p}-1}f^{1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{1}G^{\max},\upsilon\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{h_{p}}_{k=3}2^{h_{p}-k}\epsilon\cdot\left(\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},\upsilon\}+f^{k-1}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{k}G^{\max},2\upsilon\}\right)+2^{h_{p}-2}f^{2}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{2}G^{\max},\upsilon\}

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)\min\{2^{h_{p}}G^{\max},\upsilon\}\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon

To summarize, given any
c\in C
, in total
{\mathcal{O}}(\sum^{h_{p}}_{k=1}\frac{2^{h_{p}-k}}{\epsilon}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
processing time,
Ora
will return
c^{(1;1)},c^{(1;2)},c^{(1;3)},\cdots,c^{(1;2^{h_{p}})}\in G\cup\{0\}
such that

|c-\sum^{2^{h_{p}}}_{j_{1}=1}c^{(1;j_{1})}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
.

In conclude, within
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
time, we will build a linked list structure whose tail node contains an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set with cardinality of
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
for
\underbrace{G\oplus G\oplus\cdots\oplus G}_{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}=G_{\log}
. Meanwhile, with the help of this
linked list structure, an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
-time oracle
Ora
for backtracking from this approximate set to
\underbrace{G\dot{\cup}G\dot{\cup}\cdots\dot{\cup}G}_{2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}}
is derived. Then by Claim
8
, we have proved Lemma
23
for the case that
m\leq 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
.

8.2.2
Handling the case that
m>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
.

In this section, we aim to prove Lemma
23
for the case that
m>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
.

Recall that in Section
8.2.1
, we have obtained
U^{h_{p}}
, which is an
(f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon),\min\{2^{h_{p}}G^{\max},\upsilon\})
-approximate set with cardinality of
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
for
G_{\log}
. Meanwhile, we have also derived
Ora
, which is an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
-time oracle for backtracking from
U^{h_{p}}
to
\underbrace{G\dot{\cup}G\dot{\cup}\cdots\dot{\cup}G}_{2^{1+pow(1+\log_{2}\frac{1}{\epsilon})}}
. Here
h_{p}=1+pow(1+\log_{2}\frac{1}{\epsilon})
and
f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)={\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
. We have the following observation.

Observation 11
.

Given any
k=1,2,\cdots,pow(m)
,
2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
is an
\left(f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon),2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set for
2^{k}G_{\log}
. Moreover,
Ora
directly yields an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2}))
-time oracle for backtracking from
2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
to
2^{k}\underbrace{G\dot{\cup}G\dot{\cup}\cdots\dot{\cup}G}_{2^{h_{p}}}
. That is, given any
c\in 2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
, by calling
Ora
, within
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2}))
processing time, we can obtain
c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2},\cdots,c^{\prime}_{2^{h_{p}}}\in G\cup\{0\}
such that
|c-2^{k}\sum^{2^{h_{p}}}_{\iota=1}c^{\prime}_{\iota}|\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}
.

Proof.

Note that
(G_{\log})^{\max}=2^{h_{p}}G^{\max}
. We first show that
2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
is an
(f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon),2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\})
-approximate set for
2^{k}G_{\log}
. It is sufficient to observe the followings:
(i)
(2^{k}U^{h_{p}})^{\max}=2^{k}(U^{h_{p}})^{\max}\leq\left(1+f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)\right)2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}
; (ii) for any
c\in 2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
, we have
\frac{c}{2^{k}}\in U^{h_{p}}
, then there exist
c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{2^{h_{p}}}\in G\cup\{0\}
such that
|\frac{c}{2^{k}}-\sum^{2^{h_{p}}}_{\iota=1}c_{\iota}|\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}
, i.e.,
|c-2^{k}\sum^{2^{h_{p}}}_{\iota=1}c_{\iota}|\leq 2^{k}f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}
; (iii) for any
x\in 2^{k}G_{\log}\cap[0,2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}]
,we have
\frac{x}{2^{k}}\in G_{\log}\cap[0,\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}]
, then there exists
y\in U^{h_{p}}
such that
|y-\frac{x}{2^{k}}|\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}
, i.e.,
|2^{k}y-x|\leq 2^{k}f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}
.

For the second part of the observation, consider any
c\in 2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
, we have
\frac{c}{2^{k}}\in U^{h_{p}}
. Recall that
Ora
is an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
-time oracle for backtracking from
U^{h_{p}}
to
\underbrace{G\dot{\cup}G\dot{\cup}\cdots\dot{\cup}G}_{2^{h_{p}}}
. Thus in
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
time,
Ora
will return
c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2},\cdots,c^{\prime}_{2^{h_{p}}}\in G\cup\{0\}
such that
|\frac{c}{2^{k}}-\sum^{2^{h_{p}}}_{\iota=1}c^{\prime}_{\iota}|\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}
. Then we have
|c-2^{k}\sum^{2^{h_{p}}}_{\iota=1}c^{\prime}_{\iota}|\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}
. ∎∎

The following claim guarantees that towards proving Lemma
23
for the case that
m>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
, it is sufficient to consider
U^{h_{p}}\oplus 2U^{h_{p}}\oplus\cdots\oplus 2^{pow(m)}U^{h_{p}}=\oplus^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
.

Claim 10
.

Assume that
m>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
. Given
C
, which is an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),(1+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))\upsilon)
-approximate set for
\oplus^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
. Let
Ora*
be a
T
-time oracle for backtracking from
C
to
U^{h_{p}}\dot{\cup}2U^{h_{p}}\dot{\cup}\cdots\dot{\cup}2^{pow(m)}U^{h_{p}}
, that is, given any
c\in C
, in
T
processing time,
Ora*
will return
\{c^{\prime}_{0},c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2},\cdots,c^{\prime}_{pow(m)}\}
such that
|c-\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}c^{\prime}_{k}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
, where
c^{\prime}_{k}\in 2^{k}U^{h_{p}}\cup\{0\}
for every
k=0,1,2,\cdots,pow(m)
.

Then
C
is an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set of
S(\overline{M})
. Moreover,
Ora*
and
Ora
directly yiled an
{\mathcal{O}}(T+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{3})
-time oracle for backtracking from
C
to
\overline{M}
, that is, given any
c\in C
, by calling
Ora*
and
Ora
, within
{\mathcal{O}}(T+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{3})
processing time, we can compute a multiset
\overline{M}^{\prime}\subset\overline{M}
such that
|c-\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime})|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
.

Proof.

When
m>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
, elements in
\overline{M}
have the same multiplicity of
l_{M}=2m\cdot 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})+1)}
. Moreover, recall Observation
10
, we have
(G_{\log}\oplus 2G_{\log}\oplus\cdots\oplus 2^{pow(m)}G_{\log})\subset S(\overline{M})
and
S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]=(G_{\log}\oplus 2G_{\log}\oplus\cdots\oplus 2^{pow(m)}G_{\log})\cap[0,\upsilon]
. Note that
G=set(\overline{M})\subset[\frac{1}{2\epsilon},\frac{1}{\epsilon}]
and
m=\lfloor\frac{\upsilon}{G^{\min}}\rfloor
, we have

G^{\max}\leq 2G^{\min}\leq\frac{2\upsilon}{m}

Let
C
be an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),(1+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))\upsilon)
-approximate set for
\oplus^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
. We observe the followings:

•

C\subset[0,(1+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))\upsilon]
.

•

For any
c\in C
, there exists
\{c^{\prime}_{0},c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2},\cdots,c^{\prime}_{pow(m)}\}
such that
|c-\mathop{\sum}\limits^{pow(m)}_{k=0}c^{\prime}_{k}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
, where
c^{\prime}_{k}\in 2^{k}U^{h_{p}}\cup\{0\}
for every
k=0,1,2,\cdots,pow(m)
. Consider each
c^{\prime}_{k}
. Recall Observation
11
,
2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
is an
\left(f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon),2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set for
2^{k}G_{\log}
. Thus there exists
c^{(k;1)},c^{(k;2)},\cdots,c^{(k;2^{h_{p}})}\in G\cup\{0\}
such that
|c^{\prime}_{k}-2^{k}\sum^{2^{h_{p}}}_{\iota=1}c^{(k;\iota)}|\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)\cdot 2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{k}:=\{c^{(k;1)},c^{(k;2)},\cdots,c^{(k;2^{h_{p}})}\}
for every
k=0,1,2,\cdots,pow(m)
. We define
\overline{M}^{\prime}
as follows:

–

For each
g\in G
and each integer
0\leq k\leq pow(m)
, let
n^{k}_{g}
denote the multiplicity of
g
in
\mathcal{C}_{k}
, i.e.,
n^{k}_{g}=card_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}[g]
. Let
\mathcal{R}_{g}
be the multiset consists of
\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}n^{k}_{g}
copies of
g
, that is,
set(\mathcal{R}_{g})=\{g\}
and
|\mathcal{R}_{g}|=\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}n^{k}_{g}
.

–

Define
\overline{M}^{\prime}:=\dot{\cup}_{g\in G}\mathcal{R}_{g}

Notice that
\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime})=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{g\in G}g|\mathcal{R}_{g}|=\mathop{\sum}\limits_{g\in G}g\mathop{\sum}\limits^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}n^{k}_{g}=\mathop{\sum}\limits^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}(\mathop{\sum}\limits_{g\in G}n^{k}_{g}g)=\mathop{\sum}\limits^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}(\mathop{\sum}\limits^{2^{h_{p}}}_{\iota=1}c^{(k;\iota)})
. Recall that
G^{\max}\leq\frac{2\upsilon}{m}
, then we have
|\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}c^{\prime}_{k}-\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime})|\leq\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}|c^{\prime}_{k}-\sum^{2^{h_{p}}}_{\iota=1}c^{(k;\iota)}|\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}\leq mf^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)2^{h_{p}+1}G^{\max}\leq{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{3})\upsilon
. We claim that
\overline{M}^{\prime}\subset\overline{M}
, which is sufficient to observe the followings: (1).
set(\overline{M}^{\prime})\subset G=set(\overline{M})
; (2). for any
g\in set(\overline{M}^{\prime})
, the multiplicity of
g
in
\overline{M}^{\prime}
is
|\mathcal{R}_{g}|=\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}n^{k}_{g}\leq 2m\cdot 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}\frac{1}{\epsilon}+1)}
; (3). elements in
\overline{M}
have the same multiplicity of
l_{M}=2m\cdot 2^{1+pow(\log_{2}\frac{1}{\epsilon}+1)}
.

To summarize, given any
c\in C
, there exists
\overline{M}^{\prime}\subset\overline{M}
such that
|c-\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime})|\leq|c-\mathop{\sum}\limits^{pow(m)}_{k=0}c^{\prime}_{k}|+|\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}c^{\prime}_{k}-\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime})|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon.

•

For any
s\in S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]
, since
S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]=(G_{\log}\oplus 2G_{\log}\oplus\cdots\oplus 2^{pow(m)}G_{\log})\cap[0,\upsilon]
, we have
s\in(G_{\log}\oplus 2G_{\log}\oplus\cdots\oplus 2^{pow(m)}G_{\log})\cap[0,\upsilon]
. Then there
\{s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{pow(m)}\}
such that
s=\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=1}s_{k}
, where
s_{k}\in 2^{k}G_{\log}\cap[0,\upsilon]
. Consider each
s_{k}
. Note that
2^{k}G_{\log}\cap[0,\upsilon]\subset 2^{k}G_{\log}\cap[0,2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}]
, then
s_{k}\in 2^{k}G_{\log}\cap[0,2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}]
. Recall Observation
11
,
2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
is an
\left(f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon),2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}\right)
-approximate set for
2^{k}G_{\log}
, thus there exist
s^{\prime}_{k}\in 2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
such that
|s^{\prime}_{k}-s_{k}|\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}
. Let
s^{\prime}=\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}s^{\prime}_{k}
. Recall that
G^{\max}\leq\frac{2\upsilon}{m}
, then
|s-s^{\prime}|\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)2^{pow(m)+1}(G_{\log})^{\max}\leq mf^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)2^{h_{p}+1}G^{\max}\leq{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{3})\upsilon
. Note that
s^{\prime}\leq s+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon\leq(1+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))\upsilon
, we have
s^{\prime}\in\oplus^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}U^{h_{p}}\cap[0,(1+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))\upsilon]
, then there exists
s^{\prime\prime}\in C
such that
|s^{\prime\prime}-s^{\prime}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
. To summarize, for any
s\in S(\overline{M})\cap[0,\upsilon]
, there exists
s^{\prime\prime}\in C
such that

|s-s^{\prime\prime}|\leq|s-s^{\prime}|+|s^{\prime}-s^{\prime\prime}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon.

Thus
C
is an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\upsilon)
-approximate set for
S(\overline{M})
.

In the next, we show that
Ora*
and
Ora
directly yields an
{\mathcal{O}}(T+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
-time oracle for backtracking from
C
to
\overline{M}
. Here
Ora*
is a
T
-time oracle for backtracking from
C
to
U^{h_{p}}\dot{\cup}2U^{h_{p}}\dot{\cup}\cdots\dot{\cup}2^{pow(m)}U^{h_{p}}
, and
Ora
is an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
-time oracle for backtracking from
U^{h_{p}}
to
\underbrace{G\dot{\cup}G\dot{\cup}\cdots\dot{\cup}G}_{2^{h_{p}}}
. Given any
c\in C
, within
T
processing time,
Ora*
will return
\{c^{\prime}_{0},c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2},\cdots,c^{\prime}_{pow(m)}\}
such that
|c-\mathop{\sum}\limits^{pow(m)}_{k=0}c^{\prime}_{k}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
, where
c^{\prime}_{k}\in 2^{k}U^{h_{p}}\cup\{0\}
for every
k=0,1,2,\cdots,pow(m)
. Consider each
c^{\prime}_{k}
. Recall Observation
11
,
Ora
directly yields an
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2}))
-time oracle for backtracking from
2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
to
2^{k}\underbrace{G\dot{\cup}G\dot{\cup}\cdots\dot{\cup}G}_{2^{h_{p}}}
. Thus with the help of
Ora
, within
{\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2}))
processing time, we can obtain
c^{(k;1)},c^{(k;2)},\cdots,c^{(k;2^{h_{p}})}\in G\cup\{0\}
such that
|c^{\prime}_{k}-2^{k}\sum^{2^{h_{p}}}_{\iota=1}c^{(k;\iota)}|\leq f^{h_{p}}(\epsilon)2^{k}\min\{(G_{\log})^{\max},\upsilon\}
. Let
\mathcal{C}_{k}:=\{c^{(k;1)},c^{(k;2)},\cdots,c^{(k;2^{h_{p}})}\}
for every
k=0,1,2,\cdots,pow(m)
. Note that
m=\lfloor\frac{\omega}{M^{\min}}\rfloor={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon})
. The total time to obtain
\mathcal{C}_{k}
for every
k
is
{\mathcal{O}}(pow(m)\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{3})
.

Define
\overline{M}^{\prime}
as follows:

•

For each
g\in G
and each integer
0\leq k\leq pow(m)
, let
n^{k}_{g}
denote the multiplicity of
g
in
\mathcal{C}_{k}
, i.e.,
n^{k}_{g}=card_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}[g]
. Let
\mathcal{R}_{g}
be the multiset consists of
\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}n^{k}_{g}
copies of
g
, that is,
set(\mathcal{R}_{g})=\{g\}
and
|\mathcal{R}_{g}|=\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}n^{k}_{g}
.

•

Define
\overline{M}^{\prime}:=\dot{\cup}_{g\in G}\mathcal{R}_{g}

Same as the previous discussion, we have
\overline{M}^{\prime}\subset\overline{M}
and
|c-\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime})|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
. Observe that
|\mathcal{R}_{g}|
is the multiplicity of
g
in
\overline{M}^{\prime}
. We now estimate the total time for determining
|\mathcal{R}_{g}|
for every
g\in set(\overline{M}^{\prime})
. Given
k
, the time to determine
n^{k}_{g}
for every
g\in G
is
{\mathcal{O}}(2^{h_{p}})
. Then the total time to determine all
n^{k}_{g}
’s is
{\mathcal{O}}(pow(m)2^{h_{p}})
. Given
g\in G
and given
n^{k}_{g}
for every
k
, the time to determine
|\mathcal{R}_{g}|
is
{\mathcal{O}}(pow(m))
. To summarize, the total time to determine
|\mathcal{R}_{g}|
for every
g\in G
is
{\mathcal{O}}(pow(m)2^{h_{p}}+pow(m)|G|)={\mathcal{O}}(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{2})
.

In conclude, given any
c\in C
, with the help of
Ora*
and
Ora
, in overall
{\mathcal{O}}(T+\frac{1}{\epsilon}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})^{3})
processing time, we can obtain
\overline{M}^{\prime}\subset\overline{M}
such that
|c-\Sigma(\overline{M}^{\prime})|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
, moreover,
card_{\overline{M}^{\prime}}[g]
is obtained for every
g\in set(\overline{M}^{\prime})
.∎∎

In the following, we will approximate
\oplus^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
and meanwhile build an oracle for backtracking.

Approximating
\oplus^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
.

Let
\tilde{\upsilon}=(1+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon))\upsilon
. Note that
pow(m)={\mathcal{O}}(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon})
. Recall Lemma
5
, in
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}({\epsilon^{-1}})
processing time, we can compute an
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon),\tilde{\upsilon})
-approximate set with cardinality of
{\mathcal{O}}({\epsilon^{-1}})
for
\oplus^{pow(m)}_{k=0}2^{k}U^{h_{p}}
. Denote by
C
this approximate set. At the same time, we can build an
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}({\epsilon^{-1}})
-time oracle
Ora*
for backtracking from
C
to
U^{h_{p}}\dot{\cup}2U^{h_{p}}\dot{\cup}\cdots\dot{\cup}2^{pow(m)}U^{h_{p}}
, that is, given any
c\in C
, within
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}({\epsilon^{-1}})
time,
Ora*
will return
\{c^{\prime}_{0},c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2},\cdots,c^{\prime}_{pow(m)}\}
such that
|c-\sum^{pow(m)}_{k=0}c^{\prime}_{k}|\leq\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon)\upsilon
, where
c^{\prime}_{k}\in 2^{k}U^{h_{p}}\cup\{0\}
for every
k=0,1,2,\cdots,pow(m)
.

Then by Claim
10
, we have proved Lemma
23
for the case that
m>2^{1+pow(\log_{2}\frac{1}{\epsilon}+1)}
, which completes the proof of Lemma
23
, furthermore, completes the proof of Lemma
22
. Till now, we have completed the proof of Theorem
4
.

9
Conclusion

In this paper we present improved approximation schemes for (unbounded) SUBSET SUM and PARTITION. In particular, we give the first subquadratic deterministic weak approximation scheme for SUBSET SUM. However, it is not clear whether a better weak approximation scheme exists for SUBSET SUM and PARTITION. The existing results as well as our results seem to imply that PARTITION admits a better approximation scheme. This is primarily due to that the target
t
in PARTITION is
{\mathcal{O}}(\Sigma(X))
, and can be leveraged to reduce the running time of FFT. It is not clear, however, whether this is the key fact that determines the fine-grained complexity and makes weak approximating SUBSET SUM harder than PARTITION.
